<script>
const slides = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('.slide'));
let index = 0;
document.getElementById('total').textContent = slides.length;
function show(i) {
  index = Math.max(0, Math.min(slides.length - 1, i));
  slides.forEach((s, n) => s.classList.toggle('active', n === index));
  document.getElementById('cur').textContent = index + 1;
document.addEventListener('keydown', (e) => {
  if (e.key === 'ArrowRight' || e.key === 'ArrowDown' || e.key === ' ') { show(index + 1); e.preventDefault(); }
  else if (e.key === 'ArrowLeft' || e.key === 'ArrowUp') { show(index - 1); e.preventDefault(); }
  else if (e.key === 'Home') { show(0); } else if (e.key === 'End') { show(slides.length - 1); }
show(0);
</script>

  window.MathJax = {
    tex: {
      inlineMath: [['$','$']], displayMath: [['$$','$$']],
      // The tex-svg-full bundle activates every extension by default, so
      // \boldsymbol renders as true bold-italic natively. We only drop
      // 'physics' so \div keeps meaning the division sign (physics redefines
      // it as the divergence operator). \oiint/\oiiint still need a macro 
      // no esint package exists in MathJax 
 built from stacked \oint exactly
      // the way MathJax builds \iint, so they can never hard-fail a block.
      packages: {'[-]': ['physics']},
      macros: {
        oiint: '{\\oint\\!\\!\\!\\oint}',
        oiiint: '{\\oint\\!\\!\\!\\oint\\!\\!\\!\\oint}'
      }
    },
    svg: { fontCache: 'none' },
    startup: { typeset: false }
  };
</script>
<script src="tex-svg.js"></script>
<script>
  function post(msg) {
    if (window.webkit && window.webkit.messageHandlers && window.webkit.messageHandlers.math) {
      window.webkit.messageHandlers.math.postMessage(msg);
    }
  function renderMath(tex, display, color, token) {
    if (!window.MathJax || !MathJax.startup) { post({ ok: false, error: 'engine not ready', token: token }); return; }
    MathJax.startup.promise.then(function() {
      try {
        MathJax.texReset();
        var node = MathJax.tex2svg(tex, { display: display });
        var c = document.getElementById('c');
        c.innerHTML = ''; c.appendChild(node);
        var svg = c.querySelector('svg');
        if (!svg) { post({ ok: false, error: 'no svg produced', token: token }); return; }
        svg.style.color = color;
        svg.style.display = 'block';
        var valign = (svg.style.verticalAlign || '');
        var r = svg.getBoundingClientRect();
        post({ ok: true, svg: svg.outerHTML,
               x: r.left, y: r.top, w: r.width, h: r.height, valign: valign, token: token });
      } catch (e) {
        post({ ok: false, error: String((e && e.message) || e), token: token });
      }
    }).catch(function(e) { post({ ok: false, error: String((e && e.message) || e), token: token }); });
</script>

<script>
  function post(msg) {
    if (window.webkit && window.webkit.messageHandlers && window.webkit.messageHandlers.mermaid) {
      window.webkit.messageHandlers.mermaid.postMessage(msg);
    }
  let counter = 0;
  async function renderMermaid(source, theme, token) {
    try {
      mermaid.initialize({ startOnLoad: false, securityLevel: 'strict', theme: theme,
                           fontFamily: '-apple-system, Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif' });
      counter += 1;
      const id = 'rmgraph' + counter;
      const { svg } = await mermaid.render(id, source);
      const container = document.getElementById('container');
      container.innerHTML = svg;
      const el = container.querySelector('svg');
      let w = 0, h = 0;
      if (el) {
        const vb = el.viewBox && el.viewBox.baseVal;
        if (vb && vb.width) { w = vb.width; h = vb.height; }
        else { const r = el.getBoundingClientRect(); w = r.width; h = r.height; }
        // Give the SVG an explicit intrinsic size (its viewBox) and drop
        // Mermaid's `width="100%"` / inline `max-width`, so it scales responsively
        // via the CSS `max-width:100%` instead of collapsing to the 300px default
        // inside an inline-block container (which crushed wide diagrams like Gantt
        // charts into overlap).
        if (w > 0 && h > 0) {
          el.setAttribute('width', w); el.setAttribute('height', h);
          el.style.removeProperty('max-width');
        }
      }
      // Post the MODIFIED element (explicit size) 
 NOT Mermaid's original string,
      // which keeps width="100%" and collapses in an inline-block container.
      post({ ok: true, w: w, h: h, svg: el ? el.outerHTML : svg, token: token });
    } catch (e) {
      document.getElementById('container').innerHTML = '';
      post({ ok: false, error: String((e && e.message) || e), token: token });
    }
</script>
</body>
</html>

Settings Reference
Every preference, organized by section.
Theme, editor and preview fonts and sizes, line spacing, line numbers, current-line highlight, and status-bar visibility.
Soft wrap, typewriter mode, spellcheck, smart quotes and dashes, bracket pairing, and Focus-Mode paragraph dimming.
Live preview, debounce interval, scroll sync, front-matter visibility, and link behavior.
Enable rendering, theme following, source visibility, outline landmarks, visual editing, move/resize controls, default size and alignment, and security level (strict by default).
Default format, image storage, and the document structure options 
 title page, table of contents, and page numbers (honored by DOCX and PDF). For DOCX: page size, landscape orientation, front-matter inclusion, and local-image embedding. For HTML & slides: inline CSS and a custom stylesheet (remembered across launches). The include-Mermaid-source toggles for DOCX and for HTML are independent.

Reference links, images & footnotes
Define links/images once and reference them anywhere 
 including inside tables 
 and footnotes are collected into a section at the end.
Use [the editor][ed] and ![logo][lg] and a note.[^1]
[ed]: https://example.com "Remarque"
[lg]: logo.png
[^1]: This becomes a numbered footnote.
- Parent item
  - Child item
    1. Deeply nested
    2. Ordered child
- [ ] Task
- [x] Done

 left, center, or right.

GitHub/Obsidian-style admonitions render as styled boxes: `NOTE`, `TIP`, `IMPORTANT`, `WARNING`, `CAUTION` (with synonyms like `info`, `danger`).
> [!NOTE]

> Be careful here.

Privacy & Local-First
Remarque does its work entirely on your Mac.
**No** telemetry, analytics, or usage tracking
**No** network calls 
 Mermaid renders locally, never from a CDN
**No** accounts, cloud sync, or document upload
Your documents remain ordinary Markdown files in locations you choose. Diagram layout is stored as a small, optional comment that other editors simply ignore. Mermaid rendering runs at the strict security level and never executes diagram scripts.
Sandboxed, hardened & offline
Remarque runs in the macOS **App Sandbox** with a hardened runtime and makes **no network calls whatsoever** 
 every embedded web view refuses any navigation that isn't a local file, and the bundled Mermaid and math engines render entirely on-device.
The sandbox also means opening a document grants Remarque access to **that file only**. When a document displays images stored next to it, Remarque asks you to approve the document's folder the moment it opens 
 a one-time grant, remembered across launches, that also covers exporting those images. Decline and a banner above the preview offers the grant again, on your terms. Remarque never reads anything you haven't explicitly opened or approved.
Controls, tool palettes, and diagram cards carry **VoiceOver** labels; canvas nodes are reachable as accessibility elements; and the editor and preview text scale to your preferred sizes. The interface is fully keyboard-navigable.

Everything you can do from the keyboard.

Mermaid Visual Editor
Command Palette & Search
Run any action by name, and find text fast.
Press **
P** for a fuzzy-searchable list of every action: toggling panes, inserting diagrams, exporting, switching themes, and jumping to any heading. Navigate with the arrow keys, run with **Return**, dismiss with **Escape**.
Press **
F** for a floating find bar with a live match count and next/previous navigation. Matches highlight in place as you type 
 without stealing focus from the field 
 and recompute as the document changes, so Replace always acts on the text you see. Search includes Mermaid source because it's all just Markdown text. Expand the bar to **Replace** the current match or all of them at once.
**Case sensitive**, **whole word**, and **regular expression** options
Return / Shift-Return cycle through matches
Themes & Appearance
Three crafted themes that style the editor, preview, and diagrams.
**Paper** 
 a warm, calm light theme (the default).
**Midnight** 
 a premium dark theme that's comfortable, never harsh.
**Graphite** 
 a neutral, professional theme with light and dark variants.
**System** 
 follows your macOS appearance automatically.
Themes apply consistently across the editor, the preview, and Mermaid diagrams (including exported SVG and images). Adjust fonts, sizes, line spacing, and content width in Settings 
 Appearance.
square.and.arrow.up
Clean, local export to HTML, PDF, Word, RTF, slides, EPUB, and LaTeX.
Export from the toolbar, the **File** menu, or the Command Palette. A native save panel appears, the file is generated locally, and Remarque reveals it in Finder when it's done.
A complete standalone document with clean inline CSS that mirrors the preview. Mermaid diagrams are embedded as inline **SVG** rendered locally 
 never from a CDN. Point to your own stylesheet under Settings 
 Export 
 Custom CSS to fully restyle the output 
 the choice is remembered across launches.
A paginated PDF laid out with TextKit. Diagrams and math are embedded as crisp images, and the **title page**, **table of contents** (with real page numbers), and **page numbers** settings all apply. Page size follows your DOCX page-size setting (Letter or A4), with an optional **landscape** orientation (Settings 
 Export) for wide tables and diagrams.
Microsoft Word (DOCX)
Real Word styles for headings, quotes, code, and tables
Bold, italic, inline code, and clickable hyperlinks
Diagrams embedded as images (with a graceful placeholder fallback)
Optional title page, table of contents, and page numbers
Opens cleanly in Word, Pages, and LibreOffice Writer
A formatted RTF document that drops straight into any word processor or email 
 styled headings, lists, code, and links, with math and diagrams embedded as images, generated from the same typeset model as the preview.
Slide deck (HTML)
Turn a document into a self-contained slide deck: content splits into slides at each top-level heading, and the result is a single offline HTML file you navigate with the arrow keys 
 no framework or network needed.
A valid EPUB 3 e-book split into chapters at each `#` heading, with a navigation document, embedded diagrams, and your local images packaged inside the book. The XHTML is strictly valid 
 task lists and footnotes included 
 so it opens in Apple Books and pickier readers alike.
A standalone `.tex` document: headings, lists, tables, and formatting map to LaTeX, math passes through natively, and code uses a verbatim environment 
 ready to compile.
Print from the **File** menu or the keyboard. **Print Document
** (
P) prints the finished document, **Print Source
** the raw Markdown/Mermaid source, and **Print Rendered & Source
** both in one job 
 the rendered document followed by a labelled source listing. Diagrams are rendered by the bundled engine and a clean light theme is used for paper.
Importing Word, RTF & PDF
Going the other way, **File 
 Import Document
** (
I) converts an RTF, Word (`.docx`), or PDF file to Markdown and opens the result as a new document. Word packages are converted entirely in-app 
 equations, images, and numbered-list nesting all survive the trip, with images embedded so they always travel with the document. PDF text is extracted best-effort.
Control front matter, image embedding, page size, and whether to include Mermaid source 
 separately for DOCX and for HTML 
 in Settings 
 Export.
mermaid-templates
Mermaid: Templates & Types
Insert ready-made diagrams and see what renders natively.
Remarque bundles the full **Mermaid engine** and runs it in an isolated, offline web view, so **every** Mermaid diagram type renders with 100% fidelity 
 no network, no CDN. Insert templates from the **Diagram** menu, the Command Palette, or the **Mermaid Guide**.
Rendering & visual editing
**All types render fully** 
 flowchart, sequence, class, state, ER, gantt, pie, and more
**Flowchart & Sequence** also support the visual editor with its Photoshop-style tool palette
Other types render and are edited as Mermaid source
Rendering is identical in the preview and in HTML/PDF/DOCX exports, because all three use the same bundled engine.
Every diagram type can always be written and edited as Mermaid source and exported.
Mermaid: Move, Resize & Align
Lay out diagrams visually 
 stored as readable, optional metadata.
Click a diagram card in the preview to select it. A mini-toolbar and resize handle appear.
**Resize:** drag the corner handle; hold **Shift** to keep the aspect ratio. A live size readout follows your drag.
**Align:** choose left, center, or right from the mini-toolbar.
**Reset Size:** return to the default dimensions.
How it is stored
Layout is saved as a non-destructive comment directly above the diagram, so your file stays plain Markdown that any editor can read:
<!-- remarque:mermaid id="diagram-1" title="System Flow" width="860" height="480" align="center" -->
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A --> B
Metadata is only written when you actually move, resize, rename, or align a diagram. Every change is a single, undoable edit.
Mermaid: Importing
Bring diagrams in from .mmd, .mermaid, or Markdown files.
Choose **File 
 Import Mermaid Diagram
** (
I) to pull a diagram in from disk.
Pick a `.mmd`, `.mermaid`, `.md`, or `.txt` file.
Preview the source and the rendered diagram side by side.
Set a **title**, **size**, and **alignment**.
Choose where it goes: at the cursor, appended to the end, or replacing your selection.
Click **Import Diagram** 
 it appears immediately in the preview.
If a file contains raw Mermaid, it is wrapped in a fenced block automatically. If it already contains one or more `mermaid` blocks, you can choose which to import.
Mermaid: Visual Editor
rectangle.and.pencil.and.ellipsis
Build flowcharts, state and sequence diagrams by direct manipulation.
Place your cursor in a Mermaid block and press **
M** (or click **Visual Edit** on its card) to open the visual editor. It has three parts: the generated **source**, an interactive **canvas**, and an **inspector** for the selected element.
Editing a flowchart
Click **Add Node** to drop a new node, then rename it in the inspector.
Click **Add Edge**, then click a source node and a target node to connect them.
Drag nodes freely in any direction to reposition them; turn on **Snap to Grid** for tidy alignment.
Select a node to change its **shape** (rectangle, rounded, decision, circle).
Select an edge to set its **label** and **style** (arrow, line, dotted, thick).
Click **Apply** to write the generated Mermaid back into your document.
Editing a state diagram
`stateDiagram-v2` diagrams open on the same canvas: states become nodes, transitions become labelled edges, and `[*]` start/end markers appear as small circles. Edits regenerate clean `stateDiagram-v2` source 
 `state "Label" as id` aliases are preserved.
Editing a sequence diagram
Add and rename **participants** in the inspector.
Add **messages**, choose sender and receiver, edit text, and pick an arrow style.
Reorder messages with the up/down controls.
As you edit, the **generated Mermaid** updates live in the source panel. Lines the canvas doesn't edit 
 subgraphs, `style`/`classDef`/`click`/`linkStyle` directives, and exotic arrows 
 are preserved verbatim through **Apply**, and Remarque warns if an edit would orphan one of them. Choosing **Cancel** leaves your original source untouched.
Class diagrams and other rich types render with the bundled engine and are edited as source 
 Remarque never silently rewrites Mermaid it can't round-trip without losing detail.
Mermaid: Writing Diagrams
Diagrams are just fenced code blocks 
 and they render locally.
Write a diagram in a fenced block with the `mermaid` language. Remarque treats it as a first-class diagram, rendering it live in the preview 
 entirely on your Mac, with no network access.
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A[Start] --> B{Decision}
    B -->|Yes| C[Continue]
    B -->|No| D[Stop]
Flowcharts and sequence diagrams render natively and can be edited visually. Other Mermaid types render as a graceful card and remain fully editable as source.
From a card you can
Open the **Visual Editor**
**Copy** the Mermaid source or the rendered **SVG**
Toggle an inline **source** view
Select it to reveal **resize** handles and alignment controls
Insert a ready-made diagram from **Diagram 
 Insert Mermaid
**, the Command Palette, or the Mermaid Guide.
Math & Equations
Typeset LaTeX math, or build equations visually 
 fully offline.
Remarque renders LaTeX math with a bundled engine 
 no network, no setup. Write inline math between single dollar signs and display math between double dollar signs; inline equations sit on the surrounding text's baseline, so they read as part of the sentence.
Inline: $E = mc^2$ and $\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}$.
Display:
$$\int_0^\infty e^{-x^2}\,dx = \frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}$$
Dollar amounts are safe 
 `$5 and $10` is left as text, not parsed as math.
The visual equation editor
Don't know LaTeX? Choose **Format 
 Insert Equation
** (
E) or the **fx** button in the Markdown tool palette to open a Word-style editor.
Pick symbols and templates 
 fractions, roots, sums, integrals, Greek letters, operators 
 from the categorized palette.
Templates drop the cursor into the first slot so you can fill it in immediately.
Watch the live preview render as you build the expression.
Switch between **Inline** and **Display**, then click **Insert** to drop it into your document.
Math is embedded as crisp **SVG** in HTML, EPUB, and slides; as a high-resolution image in PDF, Word, and RTF; and passes through natively when you export to **LaTeX**.
A beautifully typeset rendering 
 not a web page in a window.
The preview renders your document with real native views: confident headings, readable line length, clean tables, elegant code blocks, and polished diagram cards. It updates as you type, debounced so typing always stays smooth.
Turn on **Scroll sync** in Settings 
 Preview and the editor and preview follow each other as you scroll 
 in both directions.
Headings, paragraphs, **bold**, *italic*, ~~strikethrough~~, and `inline code`
Ordered, unordered, and task lists
Tables, blockquotes, horizontal rules, and images
LaTeX math (inline & display), ==highlight==, super/subscript, and :emoji:
Callouts, footnotes, definition lists, and collapsible sections
Mermaid diagrams as interactive cards
Use **Preview Only** mode for distraction-free proofreading, or **Editor + Preview** to write and watch side by side.
Outline & Navigation
Move through large documents with a live structural map.
The Outline parses your headings into a hierarchy. Click any row to scroll the editor straight to that section. The current section is highlighted as you move the cursor.
Diagrams as landmarks
Mermaid diagrams can appear in the Outline as landmarks, labelled by their title (or first line). Toggle this in Settings 
 Mermaid 
 *Show Mermaid diagrams in outline*.
**Toggle Outline:** 
**Go to Heading:** open the Command Palette (
P) and type part of a heading.
Duplicate headings get unique, stable anchors automatically (for example `overview`, `overview-1`), so navigation and export links never collide.
Versions, Snippets & Automation
clock.arrow.circlepath
Local version history, reusable snippets, presentations, and system integration.
Remarque keeps a **local** history of each saved document 
 automatically as you edit, plus **File 
 Save Version** (
S). Open **File 
 Version History
** to see a diff against the current text and restore any snapshot 
 restoring first snapshots what you have now, so it can never lose work. Nothing leaves your Mac.
Define reusable snippets in Settings 
 Snippets. Type a snippet's **trigger** and press **Tab** to expand it (a `$0` marker sets where the cursor lands), or insert one from the Command Palette.
Presentation mode
Choose **View 
 Start Presentation** (
Return) to present the document full-screen as slides 
 they break at each top-level heading. Navigate with the arrow keys or space; press Escape to exit.
Shortcuts & Services
**Shortcuts app**: 
New Markdown Note
 and 
Append to Scratchpad
 actions.
**Services menu**: turn selected text anywhere into a new Remarque note, or append it to your scratchpad.
Workspace & Linking
Open a folder of notes, jump between them, search everything, and link notes together.
Open a folder as a workspace to browse, search, and link all your notes. Choose **File 
 Workspace** (
0) or **Open Folder as Workspace
**. The folder reopens automatically on your next launch.
Browse & Quick Open
The Workspace window lists every Markdown file in the folder. Use the **Quick Open** field to fuzzy-find a note by name and press Return to open it.
Search across files
Switch to **Search** to find a phrase across the whole project; results show the file and line, and clicking one opens it.
Wiki-links & backlinks
Link to another note with `[[Note name]]` 
 or `[[Note name|shown text]]`.
Click a wiki-link in the preview to open that note (resolved within the workspace folder).
The **Backlinks** tab lists every note that links to the one you're editing.
See the [[Architecture]] note and the [[Roadmap|project roadmap]].
A native AppKit text engine tuned for comfortable, fast writing.
The editor is built on the same text engine as the rest of macOS, so selection, undo, spellcheck, and keyboard navigation all feel exactly as you expect.
Formatting helpers
Select text and apply formatting, or insert a placeholder when nothing is selected. Everything is undoable and never mangles surrounding content.
Insert Mermaid diagram
The full set of helpers 
 headings, lists, task lists, tables, blockquotes, and rules 
 lives in the **Format** menu, the Command Palette, and the **Markdown tool palette** docked at the editor's left edge (a Photoshop-style strip you can toggle from the View menu).
Layouts: editor, preview, and outline
Work the way that suits the moment. **View 
 Toggle Preview** (
P) shows a live preview beside the editor 
 math, Mermaid, callouts, and code all render as you type, kept in sync with the source. **View 
 Toggle Outline** (
O) reveals a navigable document outline, and **Focus Mode** (
F) clears the chrome for distraction-free writing. The layout you choose is remembered per launch, and every pane is driven by the same plain-Markdown source underneath.
Live editing (WYSIWYG)
For a single-pane, render-in-place experience, turn on **View 
 Live Editing Mode** (
W) or the toggle in Settings 
 Editing. Syntax markers are concealed 
 you see **bold**, headings, and links styled rather than their `*`/`#` characters 
 and `$$
$$` math and `mermaid` fences render as inline tiles right in the text. Click into any block and it reverts to editable source instantly; click away and it re-renders. Your document is always plain Markdown underneath, so nothing about the file changes.
Syntax highlighting
Markdown structure is gently emphasized: headings stand out, markers are subdued, links are accented, and fenced `mermaid` blocks get their own diagram color. Highlighting is **incremental** 
 only the line you edit is re-styled 
 so it stays instant even in very large documents.
**Smart Editing Mode** (
S, on by default) gathers the editor's intelligence into one switch. Turn it off and Remarque behaves like a plain text editor; leave it on for the behaviors below. The fine-grained options live in Settings 
 Editing.
**Style toggles are render-aware:** clicking any wrap style 
 Bold, Italic, Code, Strikethrough, Highlight, super/subscript (or their shortcuts) 
 toggles the *whole* styled span the caret sits in, using the same grammar the preview renders, so applying a style a second time removes it instead of stacking `****` markers (even italic inside bold-italic). With no selection it styles the current word.
**Smart Headings:** with the caret at the start of a line, **Tab** cycles its heading level 
 none 
 ## 
 ### 
 none.
Press **Return** in a list and the next item's marker 
 bullet, number, or task box 
 is created for you; numbers renumber automatically. Return on an empty item ends the list.
**Tab** / **Shift-Tab** indent and outdent list lines; snippet triggers expand on Tab.
Brackets, parentheses, and quotes auto-pair around your selection.
Images: insert, paste & drop
Click the **Image** button (the photo icon in the tool palette, or **Format 
 Image**) to choose a PNG, JPEG, GIF, or SVG from an open dialog 
 any selected text becomes the alt text. You can also paste an image from the clipboard or drag one in from Finder. However it arrives, Remarque copies it into a sibling `assets/` folder and inserts the Markdown for you. Drop a web URL onto selected text to turn it into a link.
Because Remarque is sandboxed, opening a document grants access to that file only 
 not to images sitting next to it. The moment a document referencing local images opens, Remarque asks to approve the document's folder; grant it once and the images appear immediately and stay readable across launches. If you cancel, a quiet **Grant Access
** banner above the preview lets you approve later. The same grant lets exports (HTML, DOCX, EPUB, bundles) embed those images.
Press **
F** to find, and expand the panel for replace. Searches can be case-sensitive, whole-word, or full **regular expressions** with `$1` capture-group templates in the replacement.
Paste from a web page or Word and Remarque converts the rich text to clean Markdown 
 headings, bold/italic, links, code, and lists 
 instead of dumping raw HTML. Toggle it in Settings 
 Editing.
Visual table editor
Choose **Format 
 Table
** or the table button in the tool palette to build a table in a grid: set headers and cells, pick per-column alignment, add or remove rows and columns. Select an existing table first to edit it in place 
 the text around the table is preserved, and rows with extra cells are never trimmed.
**Format 
 Format Document** (
L) normalizes whitespace, list markers, and table column alignment in one undoable pass 
 never touching the inside of code or math blocks, horizontal rules, `#hashtags`, or trailing-space line breaks.
If a document changes on disk in another app, Remarque reloads it automatically 
 and asks first if you have unsaved edits, so your work is never lost.
Focus & Typewriter modes
**Focus Mode** (
F) hides the chrome and centers a readable writing column.
**Typewriter Mode** (
T) keeps the current line vertically centered as you write.
Optional **line numbers** and **current-line highlight** are in Settings 
 Appearance.
Turn on soft wrap, smart quotes, and spellcheck in Settings 
 Editing to match how you like to write.
Create your first document and learn the lay of the land in two minutes.
Remarque is a native macOS editor for serious Markdown 
 technical specs, product docs, research notes, and long prompts. Your work always stays in a plain `.md` file you fully own.
The window at a glance
**Outline** (left) 
 a live map of your headings and diagrams.
**Editor** (center) 
 a fast, native writing surface.
**Preview** (right) 
 your document, beautifully typeset.
**Status bar** (bottom) 
 word count, reading time, diagrams, save state, and cursor position.
Your first document
Press **
N** to create a new document.
Type a heading with `#`, then start writing below it.
Watch the Outline and Preview update as you type.
Press **
S** to save it anywhere as a normal `.md` file.
Press **
P** at any time to open the Command Palette and run any action by name 
 the fastest way to learn what Remarque can do.
Switching layouts
Use the layout control in the toolbar, or the **View** menu, to show just the editor, editor + preview, the full three-pane view, or preview only. Your choice is remembered between launches.

checkmark.circle.fill
Your setup sticks: the workspace folder and your custom export stylesheet now reopen automatically across launches.
Local images, unblocked: Remarque asks for folder access the moment a document needs it 
 approve once and images appear immediately, remembered across launches.
Word import, rebuilt: .docx files convert entirely in-app 
 equations and images come across, and numbered lists keep their numbering and nesting.
Math & diagrams, pixel-true: inline math sits on the text baseline, Mermaid diagrams render at their true size everywhere 
 including exported PDFs 
 and a failed render now heals itself.
PDF export now honors your Title Page, Table of Contents (with real page numbers), and Page Numbers settings.
Sturdier documents out: EPUBs package local images and satisfy strict readers, DOCX preserves nested formatting and tall diagrams, and every export writes atomically 
 a failure can never destroy the previous file.
GitHub-accurate Markdown: dozens of parsing fixes 
 snake_case stays literal, Wikipedia links with parentheses work, plus === headings, 4-backtick fences, and escaped pipes in tables.
A safety net under your text: Find & Replace tracks edits so it can't replace the wrong text, restoring a version snapshots the current text first, and Format Document, the table editor, and the Mermaid visual editor never alter what they didn't touch.
Polish everywhere: every formatting tool now toggles off on a second press, and new shortcuts

# Welcome to Remarque
*Markdown. Beautifully refined.*
Remarque is a calm, native macOS editor for serious Markdown 
 technical
specs, product docs, research notes, and long prompts. Everything stays in
a plain `.md` file you fully own.
## Structure at a glance
Use the **outline** on the left to navigate large documents. Toggle the
**preview** to see your writing typeset beautifully.
## Write with flow
- Press **
B** for bold, **
I** for italic, **
K** for a link
- Create a fenced code block with **
- Insert a diagram with **
## Diagram visually or in source
Mermaid diagrams render live and can be edited visually (**
M**):
<!-- remarque:mermaid id="diagram-welcome" title="Editing Flow" width="720" height="420" align="center" -->
```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Write[Write Markdown] --> Preview[Live Preview]
    Preview --> Diagram{Edit Diagram?}
    Diagram -->|Visually| Canvas[Visual Editor]
    Diagram -->|As source| Source[Raw Mermaid]
    Canvas --> Export[Export]
    Source --> Export
## Export without drama
Export to **HTML**, **PDF**, or **Microsoft Word** locally 
 no cloud, no
account, no network.
| Format | Best for            |
| ------ | ------------------- |
| HTML   | Sharing on the web  |
| PDF    | Printing & archives |
| DOCX   | Reviews in Word     |
> Everything is local-first. Your words never leave your Mac.
Happy writing.

<script>
const slides = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('.slide'));
let index = 0;
document.getElementById('total').textContent = slides.length;
function show(i) {
  index = Math.max(0, Math.min(slides.length - 1, i));
  slides.forEach((s, n) => s.classList.toggle('active', n === index));
  document.getElementById('cur').textContent = index + 1;
document.addEventListener('keydown', (e) => {
  if (e.key === 'ArrowRight' || e.key === 'ArrowDown' || e.key === ' ') { show(index + 1); e.preventDefault(); }
  else if (e.key === 'ArrowLeft' || e.key === 'ArrowUp') { show(index - 1); e.preventDefault(); }
  else if (e.key === 'Home') { show(0); } else if (e.key === 'End') { show(slides.length - 1); }
show(0);
</script>

  window.MathJax = {
    tex: {
      inlineMath: [['$','$']], displayMath: [['$$','$$']],
      // The tex-svg-full bundle activates every extension by default, so
      // \boldsymbol renders as true bold-italic natively. We only drop
      // 'physics' so \div keeps meaning the division sign (physics redefines
      // it as the divergence operator). \oiint/\oiiint still need a macro 
      // no esint package exists in MathJax 
 built from stacked \oint exactly
      // the way MathJax builds \iint, so they can never hard-fail a block.
      packages: {'[-]': ['physics']},
      macros: {
        oiint: '{\\oint\\!\\!\\!\\oint}',
        oiiint: '{\\oint\\!\\!\\!\\oint\\!\\!\\!\\oint}'
      }
    },
    svg: { fontCache: 'none' },
    startup: { typeset: false }
  };
</script>
<script src="tex-svg.js"></script>
<script>
  function post(msg) {
    if (window.webkit && window.webkit.messageHandlers && window.webkit.messageHandlers.math) {
      window.webkit.messageHandlers.math.postMessage(msg);
    }
  function renderMath(tex, display, color, token) {
    if (!window.MathJax || !MathJax.startup) { post({ ok: false, error: 'engine not ready', token: token }); return; }
    MathJax.startup.promise.then(function() {
      try {
        MathJax.texReset();
        var node = MathJax.tex2svg(tex, { display: display });
        var c = document.getElementById('c');
        c.innerHTML = ''; c.appendChild(node);
        var svg = c.querySelector('svg');
        if (!svg) { post({ ok: false, error: 'no svg produced', token: token }); return; }
        svg.style.color = color;
        svg.style.display = 'block';
        var valign = (svg.style.verticalAlign || '');
        var r = svg.getBoundingClientRect();
        post({ ok: true, svg: svg.outerHTML,
               x: r.left, y: r.top, w: r.width, h: r.height, valign: valign, token: token });
      } catch (e) {
        post({ ok: false, error: String((e && e.message) || e), token: token });
      }
    }).catch(function(e) { post({ ok: false, error: String((e && e.message) || e), token: token }); });
</script>

  #container { display: flex; justify-content: center; align-items: flex-start; padding: 6px; }

<script src="mermaid.min.js"></script>

<div id="container"></div>
<script>
  function post(msg) {
    if (window.webkit && window.webkit.messageHandlers && window.webkit.messageHandlers.mermaid) {
      window.webkit.messageHandlers.mermaid.postMessage(msg);
    }
  let counter = 0;
  async function renderMermaid(source, theme, token) {
    try {
      mermaid.initialize({ startOnLoad: false, securityLevel: 'strict', theme: theme,
                           fontFamily: '-apple-system, Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif' });
      counter += 1;
      const id = 'rmgraph' + counter;
      const { svg } = await mermaid.render(id, source);
      const container = document.getElementById('container');
      container.innerHTML = svg;
      const el = container.querySelector('svg');
      let w = 0, h = 0;
      if (el) {
        const vb = el.viewBox && el.viewBox.baseVal;
        if (vb && vb.width) { w = vb.width; h = vb.height; }
        else { const r = el.getBoundingClientRect(); w = r.width; h = r.height; }
        // Give the SVG an explicit intrinsic size (its viewBox) and drop
        // Mermaid's `width="100%"` / inline `max-width`, so it scales responsively
        // via the CSS `max-width:100%` instead of collapsing to the 300px default
        // inside an inline-block container (which crushed wide diagrams like Gantt
        // charts into overlap).
        if (w > 0 && h > 0) {
          el.setAttribute('width', w); el.setAttribute('height', h);
          el.style.removeProperty('max-width');
        }
      }
      // Post the MODIFIED element (explicit size) 
 NOT Mermaid's original string,
      // which keeps width="100%" and collapses in an inline-block container.
      post({ ok: true, w: w, h: h, svg: el ? el.outerHTML : svg, token: token });
    } catch (e) {
      document.getElementById('container').innerHTML = '';
      post({ ok: false, error: String((e && e.message) || e), token: token });
    }
</script>
</body>
</html>

Inline $a^2 + b^2 = c^2$ and display:
$$\sum_{i=1}^{n} i = \frac{n(n+1)}{2}$$
Diagrams & front matter
title: My Doc
```mermaid
flowchart TD
    A --> B
Privacy & Local-First
Remarque does its work entirely on your Mac.
**No** telemetry, analytics, or usage tracking
**No** network calls 
 Mermaid renders locally, never from a CDN
**No** accounts, cloud sync, or document upload
Your documents remain ordinary Markdown files in locations you choose. Diagram layout is stored as a small, optional comment that other editors simply ignore. Mermaid rendering runs at the strict security level and never executes diagram scripts.
Sandboxed, hardened & offline
Remarque runs in the macOS **App Sandbox** with a hardened runtime and makes **no network calls whatsoever** 
 every embedded web view refuses any navigation that isn't a local file, and the bundled Mermaid and math engines render entirely on-device.
The sandbox also means opening a document grants Remarque access to **that file only**. When a document displays images stored next to it, Remarque asks you to approve the document's folder the moment it opens 
 a one-time grant, remembered across launches, that also covers exporting those images. Decline and a banner above the preview offers the grant again, on your terms. Remarque never reads anything you haven't explicitly opened or approved.
Controls, tool palettes, and diagram cards carry **VoiceOver** labels; canvas nodes are reachable as accessibility elements; and the editor and preview text scale to your preferred sizes. The interface is fully keyboard-navigable.
Keyboard Shortcuts
Everything you can do from the keyboard.
Import Mermaid diagram

Mermaid Visual Editor
Command Palette & Search
Run any action by name, and find text fast.
Press **
P** for a fuzzy-searchable list of every action: toggling panes, inserting diagrams, exporting, switching themes, and jumping to any heading. Navigate with the arrow keys, run with **Return**, dismiss with **Escape**.
Press **
F** for a floating find bar with a live match count and next/previous navigation. Matches highlight in place as you type 
 without stealing focus from the field 
 and recompute as the document changes, so Replace always acts on the text you see. Search includes Mermaid source because it's all just Markdown text. Expand the bar to **Replace** the current match or all of them at once.
**Case sensitive**, **whole word**, and **regular expression** options
Return / Shift-Return cycle through matches
Themes & Appearance
Three crafted themes that style the editor, preview, and diagrams.
**Paper** 
 a warm, calm light theme (the default).
**Midnight** 
 a premium dark theme that's comfortable, never harsh.
**Graphite** 
 a neutral, professional theme with light and dark variants.
**System** 
 follows your macOS appearance automatically.
Themes apply consistently across the editor, the preview, and Mermaid diagrams (including exported SVG and images). Adjust fonts, sizes, line spacing, and content width in Settings 
 Appearance.
square.and.arrow.up
Clean, local export to HTML, PDF, Word, RTF, slides, EPUB, and LaTeX.
Export from the toolbar, the **File** menu, or the Command Palette. A native save panel appears, the file is generated locally, and Remarque reveals it in Finder when it's done.
A complete standalone document with clean inline CSS that mirrors the preview. Mermaid diagrams are embedded as inline **SVG** rendered locally 
 never from a CDN. Point to your own stylesheet under Settings 
 Export 
 Custom CSS to fully restyle the output 
 the choice is remembered across launches.
A paginated PDF laid out with TextKit. Diagrams and math are embedded as crisp images, and the **title page**, **table of contents** (with real page numbers), and **page numbers** settings all apply. Page size follows your DOCX page-size setting (Letter or A4), with an optional **landscape** orientation (Settings 
 Export) for wide tables and diagrams.
Microsoft Word (DOCX)
Real Word styles for headings, quotes, code, and tables
Bold, italic, inline code, and clickable hyperlinks
Diagrams embedded as images (with a graceful placeholder fallback)
Optional title page, table of contents, and page numbers
Opens cleanly in Word, Pages, and LibreOffice Writer
A formatted RTF document that drops straight into any word processor or email 
 styled headings, lists, code, and links, with math and diagrams embedded as images, generated from the same typeset model as the preview.
Slide deck (HTML)
Turn a document into a self-contained slide deck: content splits into slides at each top-level heading, and the result is a single offline HTML file you navigate with the arrow keys 
 no framework or network needed.
A valid EPUB 3 e-book split into chapters at each `#` heading, with a navigation document, embedded diagrams, and your local images packaged inside the book. The XHTML is strictly valid 
 task lists and footnotes included 
 so it opens in Apple Books and pickier readers alike.
A standalone `.tex` document: headings, lists, tables, and formatting map to LaTeX, math passes through natively, and code uses a verbatim environment 
 ready to compile.
Print from the **File** menu or the keyboard. **Print Document
** (
P) prints the finished document, **Print Source
** the raw Markdown/Mermaid source, and **Print Rendered & Source
** both in one job 
 the rendered document followed by a labelled source listing. Diagrams are rendered by the bundled engine and a clean light theme is used for paper.
Importing Word, RTF & PDF
Going the other way, **File 
 Import Document
** (
I) converts an RTF, Word (`.docx`), or PDF file to Markdown and opens the result as a new document. Word packages are converted entirely in-app 
 equations, images, and numbered-list nesting all survive the trip, with images embedded so they always travel with the document. PDF text is extracted best-effort.
Control front matter, image embedding, page size, and whether to include Mermaid source 
 separately for DOCX and for HTML 
 in Settings 
 Export.

Mermaid: Templates & Types
Insert ready-made diagrams and see what renders natively.
Remarque bundles the full **Mermaid engine** and runs it in an isolated, offline web view, so **every** Mermaid diagram type renders with 100% fidelity 
 no network, no CDN. Insert templates from the **Diagram** menu, the Command Palette, or the **Mermaid Guide**.
Rendering & visual editing
**All types render fully** 
 flowchart, sequence, class, state, ER, gantt, pie, and more
**Flowchart & Sequence** also support the visual editor with its Photoshop-style tool palette
Other types render and are edited as Mermaid source
Rendering is identical in the preview and in HTML/PDF/DOCX exports, because all three use the same bundled engine.
Every diagram type can always be written and edited as Mermaid source and exported.
Mermaid: Move, Resize & Align
Lay out diagrams visually 
 stored as readable, optional metadata.
Click a diagram card in the preview to select it. A mini-toolbar and resize handle appear.
**Resize:** drag the corner handle; hold **Shift** to keep the aspect ratio. A live size readout follows your drag.
**Align:** choose left, center, or right from the mini-toolbar.
**Reset Size:** return to the default dimensions.
How it is stored
Layout is saved as a non-destructive comment directly above the diagram, so your file stays plain Markdown that any editor can read:

```mermaid

    A --> B
Metadata is only written when you actually move, resize, rename, or align a diagram. Every change is a single, undoable edit.
Mermaid: Importing
Bring diagrams in from .mmd, .mermaid, or Markdown files.
Choose **File 
 Import Mermaid Diagram
** (
I) to pull a diagram in from disk.
Pick a `.mmd`, `.mermaid`, `.md`, or `.txt` file.
Preview the source and the rendered diagram side by side.
Set a **title**, **size**, and **alignment**.
Choose where it goes: at the cursor, appended to the end, or replacing your selection.
Click **Import Diagram** 
 it appears immediately in the preview.
If a file contains raw Mermaid, it is wrapped in a fenced block automatically. If it already contains one or more `mermaid` blocks, you can choose which to import.
Mermaid: Visual Editor
rectangle.and.pencil.and.ellipsis
Build flowcharts, state and sequence diagrams by direct manipulation.
Place your cursor in a Mermaid block and press **
M** (or click **Visual Edit** on its card) to open the visual editor. It has three parts: the generated **source**, an interactive **canvas**, and an **inspector** for the selected element.
Editing a flowchart
Click **Add Node** to drop a new node, then rename it in the inspector.
Click **Add Edge**, then click a source node and a target node to connect them.
Drag nodes freely in any direction to reposition them; turn on **Snap to Grid** for tidy alignment.
Select a node to change its **shape** (rectangle, rounded, decision, circle).
Select an edge to set its **label** and **style** (arrow, line, dotted, thick).
Click **Apply** to write the generated Mermaid back into your document.
Editing a state diagram
`stateDiagram-v2` diagrams open on the same canvas: states become nodes, transitions become labelled edges, and `[*]` start/end markers appear as small circles. Edits regenerate clean `stateDiagram-v2` source 
 `state "Label" as id` aliases are preserved.
Editing a sequence diagram
Add and rename **participants** in the inspector.
Add **messages**, choose sender and receiver, edit text, and pick an arrow style.
Reorder messages with the up/down controls.
As you edit, the **generated Mermaid** updates live in the source panel. Lines the canvas doesn't edit 
 subgraphs, `style`/`classDef`/`click`/`linkStyle` directives, and exotic arrows 
 are preserved verbatim through **Apply**, and Remarque warns if an edit would orphan one of them. Choosing **Cancel** leaves your original source untouched.
Class diagrams and other rich types render with the bundled engine and are edited as source 
 Remarque never silently rewrites Mermaid it can't round-trip without losing detail.
Mermaid: Writing Diagrams

 and they render locally.

 entirely on your Mac, with no network access.
```mermaid

    A[Start] --> B{Decision}
    B -->|Yes| C[Continue]
    B -->|No| D[Stop]
Flowcharts and sequence diagrams render natively and can be edited visually. Other Mermaid types render as a graceful card and remain fully editable as source.

Math & Equations
Typeset LaTeX math, or build equations visually 
 fully offline.
Remarque renders LaTeX math with a bundled engine 
 no network, no setup. Write inline math between single dollar signs and display math between double dollar signs; inline equations sit on the surrounding text's baseline, so they read as part of the sentence.
Inline: $E = mc^2$ and $\sqrt{a^2 + b^2}$.
Display:
$$\int_0^\infty e^{-x^2}\,dx = \frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}$$
Dollar amounts are safe 
 `$5 and $10` is left as text, not parsed as math.
The visual equation editor
Don't know LaTeX? Choose **Format 
 Insert Equation
** (
E) or the **fx** button in the Markdown tool palette to open a Word-style editor.
Pick symbols and templates 
 fractions, roots, sums, integrals, Greek letters, operators 
 from the categorized palette.
Templates drop the cursor into the first slot so you can fill it in immediately.
Watch the live preview render as you build the expression.
Switch between **Inline** and **Display**, then click **Insert** to drop it into your document.
Math is embedded as crisp **SVG** in HTML, EPUB, and slides; as a high-resolution image in PDF, Word, and RTF; and passes through natively when you export to **LaTeX**.
A beautifully typeset rendering 
 not a web page in a window.
The preview renders your document with real native views: confident headings, readable line length, clean tables, elegant code blocks, and polished diagram cards. It updates as you type, debounced so typing always stays smooth.
Turn on **Scroll sync** in Settings 
 Preview and the editor and preview follow each other as you scroll 
 in both directions.
Headings, paragraphs, **bold**, *italic*, ~~strikethrough~~, and `inline code`
Ordered, unordered, and task lists

A native AppKit text engine tuned for comfortable, fast writing.
The editor is built on the same text engine as the rest of macOS, so selection, undo, spellcheck, and keyboard navigation all feel exactly as you expect.
Formatting helpers
Select text and apply formatting, or insert a placeholder when nothing is selected. Everything is undoable and never mangles surrounding content.

The full set of helpers 
 headings, lists, task lists, tables, blockquotes, and rules 
 lives in the **Format** menu, the Command Palette, and the **Markdown tool palette** docked at the editor's left edge (a Photoshop-style strip you can toggle from the View menu).
Layouts: editor, preview, and outline
Work the way that suits the moment. **View 
 Toggle Preview** (
P) shows a live preview beside the editor 
 math, Mermaid, callouts, and code all render as you type, kept in sync with the source. **View 
 Toggle Outline** (

F) clears the chrome for distraction-free writing. The layout you choose is remembered per launch, and every pane is driven by the same plain-Markdown source underneath.
Live editing (WYSIWYG)
For a single-pane, render-in-place experience, turn on **View 
 Live Editing Mode** (
W) or the toggle in Settings 
 Editing. Syntax markers are concealed 
 you see **bold**, headings, and links styled rather than their `*`/`#` characters 
 and `$$
$$` math and `mermaid` fences render as inline tiles right in the text. Click into any block and it reverts to editable source instantly; click away and it re-renders. Your document is always plain Markdown underneath, so nothing about the file changes.
Syntax highlighting
Markdown structure is gently emphasized: headings stand out, markers are subdued, links are accented, and fenced `mermaid` blocks get their own diagram color. Highlighting is **incremental** 
 only the line you edit is re-styled 
 so it stays instant even in very large documents.
**Smart Editing Mode** (
S, on by default) gathers the editor's intelligence into one switch. Turn it off and Remarque behaves like a plain text editor; leave it on for the behaviors below. The fine-grained options live in Settings 
 Editing.
**Style toggles are render-aware:** clicking any wrap style 
 Bold, Italic, Code, Strikethrough, Highlight, super/subscript (or their shortcuts) 
 toggles the *whole* styled span the caret sits in, using the same grammar the preview renders, so applying a style a second time removes it instead of stacking `****` markers (even italic inside bold-italic). With no selection it styles the current word.
**Smart Headings:** with the caret at the start of a line, **Tab** cycles its heading level 
 none 
 ## 
 ### 
 none.
Press **Return** in a list and the next item's marker 
 bullet, number, or task box 
 is created for you; numbers renumber automatically. Return on an empty item ends the list.
**Tab** / **Shift-Tab** indent and outdent list lines; snippet triggers expand on Tab.
Brackets, parentheses, and quotes auto-pair around your selection.
Images: insert, paste & drop
Click the **Image** button (the photo icon in the tool palette, or **Format 
 Image**) to choose a PNG, JPEG, GIF, or SVG from an open dialog 
 any selected text becomes the alt text. You can also paste an image from the clipboard or drag one in from Finder. However it arrives, Remarque copies it into a sibling `assets/` folder and inserts the Markdown for you. Drop a web URL onto selected text to turn it into a link.
Because Remarque is sandboxed, opening a document grants access to that file only 
 not to images sitting next to it. The moment a document referencing local images opens, Remarque asks to approve the document's folder; grant it once and the images appear immediately and stay readable across launches. If you cancel, a quiet **Grant Access
** banner above the preview lets you approve later. The same grant lets exports (HTML, DOCX, EPUB, bundles) embed those images.
Press **
F** to find, and expand the panel for replace. Searches can be case-sensitive, whole-word, or full **regular expressions** with `$1` capture-group templates in the replacement.
Paste from a web page or Word and Remarque converts the rich text to clean Markdown 
 headings, bold/italic, links, code, and lists 
 instead of dumping raw HTML. Toggle it in Settings 
 Editing.
Visual table editor
Choose **Format 
 Table
** or the table button in the tool palette to build a table in a grid: set headers and cells, pick per-column alignment, add or remove rows and columns. Select an existing table first to edit it in place 
 the text around the table is preserved, and rows with extra cells are never trimmed.
**Format 
 Format Document** (
L) normalizes whitespace, list markers, and table column alignment in one undoable pass 
 never touching the inside of code or math blocks, horizontal rules, `#hashtags`, or trailing-space line breaks.
If a document changes on disk in another app, Remarque reloads it automatically 
 and asks first if you have unsaved edits, so your work is never lost.

 Appearance.

 Editing to match how you like to write.

# Welcome to Remarque
*Markdown. Beautifully refined.*
Remarque is a calm, native macOS editor for serious Markdown 
 technical
specs, product docs, research notes, and long prompts. Everything stays in
a plain `.md` file you fully own.
## Structure at a glance
Use the **outline** on the left to navigate large documents. Toggle the
**preview** to see your writing typeset beautifully.

I** for italic, **
K** for a link
- Create a fenced code block with **
- Insert a diagram with **

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    Write[Write Markdown] --> Preview[Live Preview]
    Preview --> Diagram{Edit Diagram?}
    Diagram -->|Visually| Canvas[Visual Editor]
    Diagram -->|As source| Source[Raw Mermaid]
    Canvas --> Export[Export]
    Source --> Export
## Export without drama
Export to **HTML**, **PDF**, or **Microsoft Word** locally 
 no cloud, no
account, no network.
| Format | Best for            |
| ------ | ------------------- |
| HTML   | Sharing on the web  |
| PDF    | Printing & archives |
| DOCX   | Reviews in Word     |
> Everything is local-first. Your words never leave your Mac.
Happy writing.
